```text
Key findings so far:

1. **Process status**: PID 370957 is STILL RUNNING (status S+, elapsed 07:25 = 7 minutes 25 seconds). It's `python run_batch.py --model-size 0.5B --gpu 0`

2. **Main log** (`exp_002_signals_05B.log`): Shows a NEW run starting at 2026-05-14 18:19:06. This is NOT the v4 restart (PID 370957). Wait, the log shows tasks failing one by one with stderr about model architectures (looks like a transformers warning, but the task is marked FAIL). 

The first three tasks failed, then task 4 started, but the log stopped updating at 18:28:25. The current process (PID 370957) started much more recently—around 20:55 based on the 7-minute runtime—which means there's been a fresh run initiated since that earlier log ended.

Looking at the main log more carefully, all the tasks are showing failures, and the stderr output appears to be listing model architectures rather than actual error messages, which is odd.

The critical issue is that the cache detection isn't working properly—the log shows "Skipped (completed): 0" when v4 should have skipped the 6 cached signals. I'm counting the signal files in the directories and seeing 6 unique signals across d000 and d005, with what looks like a duplicate embedding_mmlu file with different naming conventions.

The batch runner operates at the dosage/benchmark level (9 tasks = 3 dosages × 3 benchmarks), with each task running 3 signals, so "Skipped: 0" means no entire tasks were skipped even though some signals within them may have been cached. All tasks are failing with errors that look like the tail end of a transformers model architecture list—this is typically a `AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained()` failure or version mismatch issue. I need to dig into the actual error message more carefully since what's shown is just the end of a long list of supported architectures.

The process restarted about 7 minutes ago at 20:55, and the previous run at 18:19 also failed all tasks, so I should check for more detailed logs to understand what's actually going wrong.
```

Now I see the full picture. Let me read the rest of run_batch.py to understand how it detects completed tasks and how it runs subprocesses. But more importantly, let me understand:

1. The signal files that exist (7 signal files + 1 batch report):
   - d000: embedding_mmlu (2 versions!), embedding_gsm8k, embedding_arc_challenge, minkpp_mmlu, minkpp_gsm8k → 5 unique signals (+ 1 duplicate naming)
   - d005: embedding_mmlu → 1 signal
   - Total: 6 unique signals (out of 27)

2. The `merged_output_exists` function checks for `signals_merged_{benchmark}.json` — but none of those exist. The actual signal files are `signal_{type}_{benchmark}.json`. So the cache check is looking for merged files, not individual signal files, which explains why Skipped=0. The v4 update supposedly fixed per-signal caching, but I need to trace through the code to see if the cache logic is actually checking the right files.

Looking at the actual failures from the 18:19 run, tasks 1-3 failed with stderr showing model architectures, suggesting a model loading error. Since `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` is set, transformers can't download anything, so if the model or tokenizer isn't already cached locally, the task will fail.

The stderr tail looks like it's from a ValueError about an unrecognized configuration class, which is odd since Qwen2.5-0.5B should be well-supported. The batch runner captures stderr as a subprocess, but it might be truncating the output or the real error could be hidden. I need to look at how run_batch.py captures output and examine compute_signals.py more closely to find the actual root cause.

Now I understand the architecture:

1. `run_batch.py` iterates over (dosage, benchmark) combos and spawns `compute_signals.py` as a subprocess
2. The skip check uses `merged_output_exists()` which looks for `signals_merged_{benchmark}.json` — this file is only created after ALL 4 signals for a task are merged
3. Per-signal caching is inside `compute_signals.py` — individual signal files like `signal_embedding_mmlu.json` are checked before running each signal

The 18:19 run: tasks 1-3 (d000: mmlu/gsm8k/arc_challenge) all FAILED. 

The stderr is truncated to the last 500 characters, so I'm only seeing the tail end of a model architecture list — the actual error likely happened much earlier in the output. The 20:55 run is currently on task 1 of 9 and has been running for about 7 minutes, whereas task 1 failed in roughly 3.5 minutes during the 18:19 run, so the longer runtime indicates it's actually progressing further this time.

I need to check the GPU status and whether the process is still actively using it or has become a zombie, plus look for any recently modified files in the output directory to understand what's happening. I can see the full timeline now—the embedding signals go back to 16:00, with multiple datasets (MMLU, GSM8K, ARC) processed at different times, and the most recent additions being the merged and selfcritique variants from just a few minutes ago.

The current 20:55 run is actively working through its task queue, having just completed the selfcritique and merged signal generation for MMLU. The log file hasn't updated yet because the subprocess is still running—the logger only writes when the batch process finishes and processes the results.

I'm realizing there are two separate log files tracking different runs: the cron wrapper's log from the earlier 18:19 attempt (which failed due to stderr truncation), and the batch runner's own log for the current 20:55 process that's now progressing through task 2.

Looking at the signal completion status for d000, I can see that selfcritique_mmlu and signals_merged_mmlu just completed at 21:04, but most of the constat signals and some selfcritique variants are still pending or failed.

Wait, I need to reconsider — the task runs all signals for each dosage-benchmark pair, so if signals_merged_mmlu was just created, that means all required signals for d000/mmlu finished. Checking the logs, I see the signal list only includes embedding, minkpp, and selfcritique — constat isn't part of this run. So across the 3 dosages and 3 benchmarks, there are 27 total signals to complete. For d000, mmlu is now fully done with all 3 signals merged, but gsm8k is only 2 out of 3 complete, and arc_challenge is still mostly pending.

Looking at d005 and d100, they're barely started — d005 has just one signal done across all benchmarks, and d100 doesn't even have a directory yet. Out of 9 possible completed tasks, only d000/mmlu is finished. The run is actively progressing through the queue, having just completed task 1 and moving onto task 2. The earlier skip count of zero makes sense because no merged files existed at that point in the run.

The merged file timestamp at 21:04 is from this current run, which started at 20:55 and has been executing for about 7-8 minutes now. I should check if the process is still actively running and what the GPU usage looks like.

Wait, I'm noticing something odd with the 18:19 run — all the tasks show as FAILED with truncated stderr, yet signal files were successfully generated from that same run. How can a task fail but still produce output files?

Oh, I see what's happening: `compute_signals.py` processes signals sequentially, so some signals might complete successfully and write their files to disk before a later signal (like selfcritique) crashes and causes the subprocess to exit with a non-zero return code. The batch runner interprets that non-zero exit as a task failure, but the individual signal files that succeeded before the crash are already persisted. Since the per-signal cache checks for existing output files and skips recomputation, those successful signals won't be rerun on retry even though the overall task is marked as failed.

Looking at the 18:19 run logs, only 4 tasks started with the first 3 showing failures and task 4 beginning but never completing — the process must have crashed during or right after task 4, which explains why the signal files from that run are timestamped later than the main log entries.

The signal files timestamped 20:49-20:50 (signal_embedding_gsm8k, signal_minkpp_gsm8k) don't match the 18:19 run timeline, suggesting either the 18:19 run continued processing tasks 4 and beyond without updating the main log, or there was a separate run between 18:28 and 20:55 that generated those files. Since the main log file appears to get overwritten with each new run, the earlier entries from the 18:19 run were likely lost when the next run started.

Looking at the file modification times more closely, both run_batch.py and compute_signals.py were edited at 20:50, which aligns with when those signal files were created. This suggests there was a test run or manual execution around 20:49-20:50 where code changes were being tested, followed by the current run starting at 20:55 after the code was finalized.

The key takeaway is that the current process (PID 370957) has been running for over 7 minutes and just completed task 1 of 9 at 21:04, producing the merged MMLU signals file. So far I've got 7 individual signals across the datasets and 1 fully completed task, with the 18:19 run having failed due to non-zero return codes from the tasks.

Looking at the stderr output, the error message was truncated, but the pattern suggests selfcritique was consistently failing on every task in that earlier run—even though embedding and minkpp signals were succeeding. The v4 fixes (reducing max_tokens from 256 to 64) appear to have resolved the selfcritique issue, since it just produced output successfully at 21:04. The current run should now proceed through all remaining tasks without hitting the same bottleneck.
